## 2009 United States National Championship

![](https://i.ormos.uk/de/be/debeca5e8932c33d6d8cf17b601a48e849a159786a7fbb692d72e51775820b09.jpg)

The United States has a 2009 National Champion! The champion was crowned after a monstrous Top 8. Mark Hendrickson, who managed to make it through half of the Swiss rounds without a single blemish on his record, was joined by newcomer Drew Dumanski of Texas. They fell in the Quarterfinals, along with Grand Prix-Los Angeles Top 8 competitor Brett Piazza, and Rookie of the Year candidate Brian Robinson.

The Semifinals saw Brad Nelson and Todd Anderson fall to eventual finalists Adam Yurchick and Charles Gindy. Nelson, the famous **Magic Online** aficionado known as “FFFreak” brought a Conley Woods special to the tournament, tuned to beat Cruel Control. With his 3rd/4th playoff opponent Todd Anderson having full information about his deck, however, his tricks were no good and Anderson managed to earn the final slot for the team.

That left just one match to play out. In a powerful back and forth affair between Adam Yurchick and Charles Gindy, the Pro Tour-Hollywood champ Gindy managed to come back from a haymaker of an [Identity Crisis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Identity+Crisis) out of Yurchick in the second game. The two players wrestled the match to a fifth and final game, but Adam just couldn’t hold on. Your 2009 United States National Champion is Charles Gindy!

## Quarterfinals: Brian Robinson (Sygg Jund) VS Todd Anderson (Cruel Control)

*by Bill Stark*

Brian Robinson entered the 2009 U.S. Nationals tournament as a leading contender for the Rookie of the Year title. With a win against his opponent Todd Anderson in the Quarterfinals, he’d leave the weekend at the top of those standings. Anderson, his opponent, hailed from Alabama and was a regular columnist for the **Magic** website Starcitygames.com. He was hoping to quash Robinson’s Rookie of the Year hopes, at least for this weekend, but he did have to contend with the reality that he was facing a tough competitor who already had a Premier level Top 8: Brian broke through at Pro Tour-Kyoto earlier in the season.

On the play, Robinson’s aggressive Jund deck missed the opening turns with no creatures. On the third turn he had [Boggart Ram-Gang](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boggart+Ram-Gang) , and thanks to Vivid lands Todd couldn’t even use [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) to counter, having only a single land untapped. Anderson fell to 17, and had to watch as Brian’s follow-up to the Ram-Gang was a [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) .

![](https://i.ormos.uk/45/8e/458e8bd71407a38edd2c08f81b0556fc08c91e7454d9a2083d908d0a1b041791.jpg)*Pro Tour-Kyoto Top 8er Brian Robinson.*“Fallout. Come on Fallout!” Todd chanted, jokingly. Should his opponent reveal the *Conflux* instant [Volcanic Fallout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Fallout) for the Elf’s cascade, Robinson would have to figure out whether he was better off casting the card or not. Instead, [Sygg, River Cutthroat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sygg%2C+River+Cutthroat) made its way to the table au gratis, but was countered by [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) . The attack from Robinson took Todd to 11, and the Five Color player wiped the board clean with [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) . Brian rebuilt with [Sign in Blood](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sign+in+Blood) followed by [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) , watching as his opponent missed a land drop, stuck on three. Robinson’s side of the battlefield had virtually exploded as he had a whopping six on the table.

A second [Sign in Blood](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sign+in+Blood) from Brian allowed him to cast [Sygg, River Cutthroat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sygg%2C+River+Cutthroat) , but Todd had [Agony Warp](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Agony+Warp) for [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) . Robinson cast [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) to drop his opponent to 8, drawing a card in the process. Anderson was able to keep the table clear of additional creatures beyond the Sygg, but Brian landed a third copy of [Sign in Blood](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sign+in+Blood) , then a [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) to draw from his Sygg. He was allegedly the beatdown deck, but he had drawn far more cards in the match than his opponent. Todd Anderson fell to a paltry 4 life.

Todd cast a [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) , finally reaching six mana and glumly said “Well, if you got it, you got it.” He was referring to a [Bituminous Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bituminous+Blast) from Robinson and sure enough, the second Todd tried to pass the turn, Robinson responded with the instant. “I think I’m dead to pretty much anything...” Todd bemoaned, referring to a free spell Brian would get from the cascade. When Robinson revealed [Boggart Ram-Gang](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boggart+Ram-Gang) , Todd conceded.

Brian Robinson 1, Todd Anderson 0

[Sygg, River Cutthroat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sygg%2C+River+Cutthroat) was the first creature onto the battlefield in the second game and it came from Brian Robinson. His opponent didn’t have a problem with it landing, instead using Brian’s end of turn step to [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) in order to draw two cards. When the Massachusetts resident cast [Boggart Ram-Gang](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boggart+Ram-Gang) , Anderson again let it resolve, killing it with a [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) that became 1/1 from the block thanks to wither.

Todd wasn’t missing lands for the second game of the match, casting [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) to deal with his opponent’s Sygg. Calm in stature, Brian Robinson serenely untapped and cast [Blightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightning) , choosing to redirect the damage from the sorcery to his opponent’s planeswalker. [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) hit the battlefield for Brian, and he revealed [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) , choosing to target his opponent’s 1/1 [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) . Anderson had the perfect trump: [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to counter the Elf and bounce his defender. It was a nice one-card solution to a two-card threat.

Untapping, Anderson plucked the top card of his library and quickly offered up a “Thank you!” as he tapped 3BRG and cast [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) . Robinson, now in trouble, could only follow up with a [Blightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blightning) , but he fast needed a [Bituminous Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bituminous+Blast) or some other form of removal to deal with the double Dragons he was facing down. The 4/4s bashed Brian to 12, and Todd passed the turn with six mana up.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/46/f0/46f0eeeb183580fdbbcd3abb9198305ff37f4d9762d66091cf11b98843fe896c.jpg)*Brian Robinson reacts to a Todd Anderson play.*Robinson wasted no time untapping and casting [Bituminous Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bituminous+Blast) . The instant revealed [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) , dealing 5 to Todd. That put him at 15 life, but an [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) in response to the removal spell couldn’t find him a counter and he was forced to allow his Dragon token to die. He did make it to seven mana, opening up the possibility of a [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) , but he simply attacked Brian to 8 and passed the turn. Robinson sent with [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) , which was munched by Anderson’s re-cast [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) . Post-combat, two [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) s were aimed at the [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) , but Anderson used [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to bounce the Dragon and draw a card in response to the second copy of the ***Magic** 2010* instant. Todd re-cast the Dragon giving his opponent a single draw step to find a solution. When he didn’t, they were on to the third game with the match tied.

Brian Robinson 1, Todd Anderson 1

“Finally!” Brian Robinson exclaimed as he took his second turn of the third game. “My friend and yours...” With a smile from its caster, [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) entered the battlefield on the second turn for the first time in the match. When Robinson tried to follow up with [Sign in Blood](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sign+in+Blood) the next turn, Anderson had [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) to counter. He won the clash, but considering his opponent was playing [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) , that was actually not a good thing. If the 2/2 wound up in his opponent’s graveyard, it could potentially be lethal late in the game.

[Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) traded for [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) , leaving the scores tied at 16-all. Brian cast [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) , but missed a land drop. The 3/3 Stag was a prominent member of the ***Magic** 2010* rare class, and had seen plenty of action throughout the weekend. “Don’t like that...” Anderson whispered under his breath. Robinson attacked him to 13, and Anderson used [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) to kill the 3/3 Stag. The battlefield soon saw a second copy of the 3/3 land off the cascade from [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) .

Anderson had a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) to chump the [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) alongside his [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) , but Robinson trumped after drawing his fifth land: [Bituminous Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bituminous+Blast) cascaded into [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) killing both of his opponent’s blockers. That allowed Brian to attack unimpeded, putting Anderson down to 7. A second attempted all-in attack saw Anderson [Fog](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fog) with [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) , tapping Brian’s team and drawing a card in the process. He then cast [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) to get back in the game, shooting the Sable Stag and crawling up to 10 life. His opponent, who had continued solidifying his creature base with a [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) , was still threatening a sizeable crack back.

[Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) from Brian killed Ajani, and he cast [Boggart Ram-Gang](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boggart+Ram-Gang) to attack for lethal. Anderson made no motion to scoop, and when Brian attempted to pump his [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) , the Alabaman revealed why: [Agony Warp](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Agony+Warp) to kill the 2/2 Leech and shrink [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) . He took only 3, falling to 7, then untapped and cast [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) . “Get there Dagron!” He said, using a colloquialism to refer to his 4/4.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/3e/58/3e58c5e3d1d0769a37ba1520032bd1e2a0bb1a9607f21126d0546360302382c7.jpg)*Magic writer Todd Anderson.* [Sygg, River Cutthroat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sygg%2C+River+Cutthroat) entered the battlefield for Robinson, who was able to cantrip immediately with a [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) , dropping Todd to 4. Anderson still needed to do some maneuvering with his life so low; [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) would be a pretty hot topdeck. Instead Todd did some mental calculations upon taking his own turn, attacking Brian with his Dragon token. Robinson untapped, drew a card, and moved into his attack step. He sent his entire team, Ram-Gang, Bloodbraid, and Sygg, and Anderson cast [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) to block. He took 1 from Sygg, falling to 3, but survived the turn with a huge exhalation of breath. He attacked back to put Brian at 2 life, and when Brian tried to take the game with a topdecked [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) , Todd revealed [Essence Scatter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Essence+Scatter) to seal it up in his favor.

Todd Anderson 2, Brian Robinson 1

[Sygg, River Cutthroat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sygg%2C+River+Cutthroat) was Brian Robinson’s two-drop for the fourth game of the match, and he spent his third turn casting [Sign in Blood](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sign+in+Blood) to make sure he had a land. [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) entered the battlefield for Brian Robinson, but he opted not to attack with his Sygg for fear of losing it to [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) . Todd Anderson cast [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) to draw two cards, and Brian responded with [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) .

“Um, this is end step...” Todd said, unsure why his opponent was casting the instant.

“So...this triggers, right?” Brian replied, motioning at his [Sygg, River Cutthroat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sygg%2C+River+Cutthroat) . He gave no sign of regret about resolving the Bolt when it was revealed the 1/3 would not, in fact, trigger, and Robinson chugged right along with a [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) and [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) to put his opponent at 12 life the following turn. When he turned them both sideways after they lost summoning sickness, Todd cast [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) and traded it with [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) . Robinson had [Sign in Blood](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sign+in+Blood) for the second time in the game to re-stock his hand post-combat, but a second try on [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) met its demise at [Essence Scatter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Essence+Scatter) .

[Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) hit for Todd Anderson, a techy sideboard choice he had picked up from watching Shuhei Nakamura win at Japanese Nationals the previous weekend. It was set on [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) , and Robinson tried to ace it with [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) on his turn. Anderson cast [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to [Dismiss](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismiss) it, but allowed his opponent’s [Sygg, River Cutthroat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sygg%2C+River+Cutthroat) to resolve. Untapping for his own turn, the Starcity writer revealed why as he cast [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) , dropping it to one loyalty counter to kill the 1/3 and pad his life total to 13. Brian’s [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) moved to the red zone to take out the planeswalker, unable to hit Todd thanks to [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) .

[Boggart Ram-Gang](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boggart+Ram-Gang) hit the battlefield for Robinson, but it was shut down by a second [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) from Anderson, who set the enchantment on the 3/3 Goblin. After complaining about not being able to draw the card throughout the match, Todd Anderson finally hit [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) and plopped it onto the table. The play brought some responses from Robinson, who cast [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) in anticipation of being forced to discard the card. The turn ended with Anderson sitting on a [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) in his hand, at 12 life, and plus three cards while his opponent was at 9 life and minus an [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) .

Robinson cast [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) , fetching up a free [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) , but Anderson untapped and cast [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) to kill the 3/2. His opponent used a combat step to send both of his creatures at the planeswalker, and Todd cast a [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) end-of-turn to block during the next combat step. His follow-up was a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) , which entered the battlefield fully instead of being evoked. Brian attacked with [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) to trade with [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) , but Anderson untapped, attacked with [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) to put his opponent at 5, and revealed a second [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) . Brian Robinson nodded solemnly, extending his hand in defeat.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/76/e2/76e20ea4bf2bea89ea9ecdd2bb7ac0c1d08666bdda5dceaa40c4a3bbbb5b2b7c.jpg)*Todd Anderson gets some love from the crowd as he moves on to the Semifinals.*

Todd Anderson 3, Brian Robinson 1

## Quarterfinals: “I Think My Mulldrifters are Better” - Mark Hendrickson (The Spanish Inquisition) vs. Charles Gindy (Cruel Control)

*by Scott Johns*

![](https://i.ormos.uk/59/61/59619afa9c36c633e11d3ec275e023dd9ecd7e8979b6a53b5b11762a287357df.jpg)*Mark Hendrickson*Mark Hendrickson came into this round running a very impressive Constructed streak of 11-0. Running an innovative red-white control deck, he went 5-0 in a grinder to gain entry to the main event, and then went 6-0 in that event’s constructed rounds. After that Hendrickson made Top 8 by taking a draw and conceding to his current opponent Gindy. But, chatting with Gindy during the pre-game, Hendrickson admitted this was a nightmare Top 8 for his deck, and that the match-up against Gindy’s Cruel Control deck was particularly rough. Gindy just smiled, continuing to shuffle up while looking pretty comfortable all things considered.

Hendrickson chose to play first for the opening game. It was all vivid lands for both sides across the opening turns. Gindy quipped, “Going to be an action-packed game clearly. Plus you having no creatures and all.”

Hendrickson cast an [Armillary Sphere](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Armillary+Sphere) , joking that this was his red-white deck’s version of a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) . “I think my [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) s are better,” laughed Gindy.

Gindy got his own card advantage going with help from an [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) , and both decks settled into the business of getting mana into play and more cards in hand. Gindy stopped at 6 lands, having just a [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) in play. Hendrickson had the first big threat in the form of [Call the Skybreaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Call+the+Skybreaker) . A rogue card that has really flown under the radar, it’s particularly good in this kind of situation where Hendrickson is under no pressure and able to get plenty of lands going for the retrace ability.

But, Gindy wasn’t without answers. A [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) hit the first one, revealing a second [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) for later. A [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) tried to solidify Gindy’s position, but Hendrickson had [Hallowed Burial](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hallowed+Burial) to send all the creatures to the bottom of Gindy’s deck. Next a Gindy [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) went down, wiped out by a [Martial Coup](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Martial+Coup) that left five soldiers on the battlefield.

Gindy cast a [Volcanic Fallout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Fallout) , pointing out that he hadn’t expected to be using that card much in this match. Hendrickson now had a [Goblin Assault](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Assault) in play, but was having trouble pulling ahead as his attempts to [Call the Skybreaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Call+the+Skybreaker) went down to multiple [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) s. At this point Gindy was up to seven cards while Hendrickson was completely out of gas. Gindy cast another [Volcanic Fallout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Fallout) to control the goblin outbreak while also dropping Hendrickson to 1 after some swings from a [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) . Hendrickson tried getting back into the game but a third [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) from Gindy locked things up so that the dragon could swing in for the final blow.

Charles Gindy 1, Mark Hendrickson 0

### Game 2

Hendrickson led with a [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag+) but Gindy came back with one of his own. Gindy’s went down to a [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) while Hendrickson’s caught fire thanks to [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) . Hendrickson’s [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) took Ajani out and a [Goblin Assault](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Assault) joined the team to start getting some pressure on.

Gindy had another Stag to try holding the fort, but [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) put an end to that plan. Next Gindy went for [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) to empty Hendrickson’s hand, but this too was answered, eating a [Guttural Response](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Guttural+Response) . Stag #3 for Gindy managed to stick a bit, and when Hendrickson went for the big move with [Identity Crisis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Identity+Crisis) Gindy was able to save himself with [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) . Putting his own big move on, Gindy played out a [Violent Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Violent+Ultimatum) to send Hendrickson teetering, allowing a Dragon Broodmate finished the job. Both players had cast some of their biggest spells, but Gindy’s were just fitting the match better so far.

Charles Gindy 2, Mark Hendrickson 0

### Game 3

![](https://i.ormos.uk/6b/60/6b600404bfe0db519a099ca7075a1f1a85884cf1b4db2efc5b71de54187b7458.jpg)*Charles Gindy*Talking about how bad this bad match-up was for his deck, Hendrickson chose to draw first this time, figuring his best chance was if Gindy had a shaky opening. Hendrickson opened with a mulligan but was able to put some offense together with the help of double [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) . Double Stag got in the way but double [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) handled that nicely.

Gindy was the first in with a haymaker, casting [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) . When Hendrickson started tapping his own six mana Gindy cried out “Don’t do it!”

Hendrickson chuckled. “What, wreck your hand?”

Gindy winced. “Yeah, that. Don’t do that.”

Hendrickson smiled. “No, it’s just this.” As [Obelisk of Alara](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Obelisk+of+Alara) entered the battlefield Gindy was visibly relieved. “Oh, ok. I’m more ok with that.”

The life totals were close at this point, 14-12 in favor of Gindy. He bought time with a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to bounce the Obelisk, but when he tried it a second time Hendrickson had a [Guttural Response](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Guttural+Response) . Both of Hendrickson’s [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) s kept pounding in, but the dragons dropped him to one on the final turn once Hendrickson had bought just enough live to survive the turn thanks to his obelisk. Unfortuantely for him, Gindy had the [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) to finish things off, pointing the burn ability straight at Hendrickson’s life total.

It had been an impressive run, but here in the quarterfinals Hendrickson took his first loss of the weekend with his deck, ending his event while Gindy moved on to the semi-finals and one last hurdle to making the US team. Waiting for him there will be Todd Anderson.

Charles Gindy wins 3-0

## Quarterfinals: Breaking the Mirror - Adam Yurchick (Cruel Control) vs. Drew Dumanski (Cruel Control)

*by Brian David-Marshall*

![](https://i.ormos.uk/3c/06/3c06bc1927560d65131ac455a548b838c63bbf73597c04dee08f30bd746aeb14.jpg)With a slightly later start than the previous two days of tournament play I stayed up late into the night playing Magic. One of the last players still in the hall as they were locking up were Adam Yurchick and Cedric Phillips testing Adam’s semifinal match-up against either Brad Nelson or Brett Piazza. Adam had the luxury of knowing exactly what deck he would be facing since that quarterfinal pairing featured matching sets of 75 cards. This is not to imply that Adam had not tested his quarterfinal match-up against Drew Dumanski. They had played out the sideboarded games many times over; two [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) s after sideboarding, maindeck [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) , plus [Identity Crisis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Identity+Crisis) and [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) from the board left Adam confident that they would need to be prepared for the semis. He was concerned about a couple of cards from Dumanski like Lilliana Vess, [Scepter of Fugue](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scepter+of+Fugue) , and a [Pithing Needle](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pithing+Needle) and his record against the deck in games that counted this weekend.

“We played the first round of Standard after the drafts,” said Adam as the two players sat down to determine which of them would not have a shot at a U.S. National team berth. “He won.”

“I don’t feel good about it though,” said Drew, “You were mana-screwed in Game 3.”

Drew had not tested the match-up as extensively. The 18-year old explained about this mirror match of Cruel Control decks, “I have played this match-up a lot. I know my sideboard plan.”

### Game 1

![](https://i.ormos.uk/dc/b1/dcb176eb60f44fc5b7c985da8f81bb5420f654ad14c619a96bc57b2d822013c0.jpg)Adam was on the draw and attempted the first play of the game with Drew only having two open mana -- [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) . Drew had the [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) . Adam revealed [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) to Drew’s land, which drew him two cards at the end of the next turn. When next we pick up the action both players had nine lands and a [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) as the two players crafted their hands for the eventual endgame. Adam continued to draw lands while Drew had to discard [Volcanic Fallout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Fallout) , two [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) s, [Hallowed Burial](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hallowed+Burial) and [Agony Warp](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Agony+Warp) while “stalled” on nine lands. Adam decided to break the seal with a [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) rather than discard his. He had significantly out-mana’d his opponent and could afford to get into a fight. Drew was not willing to accept the challenge and Adam reached for his dragon token.

Adam looked to clear an airstrip for his dragons with [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) on [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) but Drew countered it with [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) bouncing the token. Adam nodded and then sent the other half into the [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) . he played [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) after combat and Drew’s [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) was [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) d. When the dust settled Adam was discarding and Drew had two cards. One of them was [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) but Adam still had enough mana left to [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) that. [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) finished off Drew’s hand. It is hard to call someone “mana-screwed” with nine lands in play but that was how this key sequence felt.

Adam redeployed his [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) and Drew attempted to rebuild his hand/get a chump blocker with [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) . Adam played another [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) and when Drew did not cast the last [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) of that turn he scooped.

Adam Yurchick - 1 Drew Dumanski - 0

### Game 2

![](https://i.ormos.uk/de/70/de70e5af30404e7e6d76e2e1396d44deaccabff4001868beecbcc48cf130313f.jpg)Adam’s sideboarded out 3 [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) , 3 [Volcanic Fallout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Fallout) , 2 [Hallowed Burial](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hallowed+Burial) , and a Doomblade for [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) , 2 [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) , 2 [Identity Crisis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Identity+Crisis) , 2 [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) , and 2 [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) . Adam sided out a similar set of cards for Lilliana Vess, [Pithing Needle](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pithing+Needle) , [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) s, and Stags.

Both players kept their hands and Drew kicked off the action with [Scepter of Fugue](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scepter+of+Fugue) . Adam dropped [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) naming Scepter and evoked [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) a turn later to find lands. Drew played [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) only to have it bolted by [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) . Drew untapped to name Ajani with his own [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) . Adam was still able to lock down a land but he was stalled on four. When Drew attempted to draw cards with [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) , Adam [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) ed the spell and his land. Now it was Adam’s turn to discard while Drew drew plenty of lands over the next handful of turns. Drew played a second [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) also naming the now ultimate-enabled Ajani.

Adam played an end of turn [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) , and Drew was fine with it as long as it did not interact with him or his board, “Drawing two?” Adam nodded and the two cards got the lands flowing for the Grand Prix Philadelphia finalist. The two players silently bided their time as Ajani’s loyalty was nealry great enough to fire off twice. Adam began to work his way toward that dream and destroyed one [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) with an [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) . Drew untapped into [Pithing Needle](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pithing+Needle) -- a card that Adam had joked was going to get him the night before. There was no counter from Adam and Drew Needled Ajani. Adam [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) ed the remaining Halo at the end of Drew’s turn.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/48/e8/48e8edc0f5cfb60f863dd5fd797d0cfdf959b30cc0a53c31bcdb4904f2d878bd.jpg)Adam deliberated about getting into a fight over bouncing the Needle but remained patient. Once he bounced that Needle he could blow up all of Drew’s land and the game would end soon thereafter. Adam instead played a [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) and passed the turn, waiting for his opponent to do something he could get into a tussle over. Drew offered up [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) and that was the opening Adam was looking for. “That’s fine,” said Adam knowing that the end game had arrived. Both players had eight mana available to them which would mean Adam was on the losing end of [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) Fight but he had brought extra bullets to this gunfight. At the end of Drew’s turn he attempted [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to bounce the Needle and draw a card. Drew countered with his own [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) -- attempting to bounce Ajani -- but Adam had a [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) for it and the [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) that followed.

Adam got to untappageddon with seven loyalty still in the tank. [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) , also on that turn was enough for Drew to scoop.

“I think I made a mistake that game...not killing your [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) early,” said Drew, who had used an early [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) to draw two cards while his [Scepter of Fugue](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scepter+of+Fugue) was blanked by the white enchantment.

Adam Yurchick - 2 Drew Dumanski - 0

### Game 3

Drew kept his openers but Adam sent a one-lander back for something better.

While they shuffled, Drew continued to chew on not killing the Scepter. “Were you worried about the Scepters after you saw them?” he asked Adam in regard to his overnight playtesting session.

“Yeah...they cost two.”

![](https://i.ormos.uk/bc/11/bc11b984174d3884e8fa2af3886dcd661d7bb565a05184cba51dc14f8728b05a.jpg)By now the two players were off and Adam was able to [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) a turn three Scepter. He untapped into Jace and greedily drew one card for himself only. Drew evoked [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) to keep pace. Adam one-upped him by paying full price his [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) and went up three cards that turn. Drew had no play and took two from the flier a turn later. Adam ticked the loyalty up on Jace and they both drew. Adam’s card advantage mounted as he [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) ed for two cards at the end of turn. Drew stuck a Scepter but at this point it was just going to save Adam the trouble of discarding at the end of his own turn.

Adam leveraged his advantage to force through an [Identity Crisis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Identity+Crisis) . Drew did his best to scratch his way back out of his existential miasma with [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) into [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) but Adam was loaded for bear and played [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) without any resistance. He [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) ed Drew’s lone card after it was drawn during the draw step. Drew extended his empty hand in defeat.

Final result: Adam Yurchick defeated Drew Dumanski in three straight games to advance to play the winner between Brad Nelson and Brett Piazza

## Quarterfinals: Brad Nelson (Jund Mannequin) vs. Brett Piazza (Jund Mannequin)

*by Kelly Digges*

The start of this Nationals Quarterfinal saw both players sit down looking refreshed and ready. Brad Nelson got his start as a longtime **Magic Online** terror, but with a 9th-place finish at Pro Tour–Honolulu and now a Nationals Top 8, it seems safe to say he’s the real deal in meatspace as well. Brett Piazza, who hails from Colorado, broke the premier level Top 8 barrier earlier this year in GP–Los Angeles.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/af/24/af240f9928f6d3ef8f55473b6c546428945ebed26e5d891ad8b772be951eb9d6.jpg)*Brett Piazza*Both Nelson and Piazza came to the table with a Jund [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) deck designed by PT–Honolulu Top 8er Conley Woods. With Conley finishing 12th (with an 8-0 Standard record) and Brad and Brett both in the Top 8, Jund Mannequin has an incredible record on the weekend. But with only three people playing the deck, their extensive pre-tournament testing did not include the mirror match—exactly what Nelson and Piazza now faced.

Piazza’s mirror match experience remained theoretical, but Nelson had tested the mirror (along with the other Top 8 match-ups) Saturday night into the early morning with Woods and found that all 75 cards in the main deck and sideboard are potentially relevant in the mirror match. The match hinges on sideboarding, with the optimal decisions depending on what you expect your opponent to sideboard. Of course, what he sideboards depends on what he thinks you’ll sideboard ....

Nelson showed no hint that these mind games were taking their toll, but Piazza looked a little grim

“You look so serious,” said Nelson as the two shuffled, then together, chuckling, they chorused: “Why so serious?”

Piazza shrugged. “I’m taking it seriously this time,” he said, back to business. “No joking around up here.”

“Well, you’ve got to enjoy it while you’re here,” replied the jovial Nelson.

“For sure, for sure,” said Piazza ... but he still wasn’t smiling.

### Game 1

Both competitors kept their seven-card hands, and started the game off cluttering the board with Vivid lands and [Reflecting Pool](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reflecting+Pool) s. Nelson, on the draw, had the first spell with [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) , but Piazza answered with [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) . A cascaded [Volcanic Fallout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Fallout) killed the Finks once, and then the Elf crashed in and traded with the diminished persist creature.

Nelson evoked [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) , and Piazza added a [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) of his own, but Nelson had [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) , at full price, to kill the Finks the first time. So far, both competitors’ life totals were going up instead of down. Piazza’s second [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) spun up [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) , and so did Nelson’s first one the next turn. Neither Elf attacked, and Piazza’s [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) kept [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) back as well.

Piazza had no play, and Nelson, on his turn, attacked with [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) . Piazza reached for his own copy of the Zombie Leech, then paused and started doing the math. He picked up the Leech, put it down again, and finally declared no blocks. His end-step [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher) —no doubt the cause of some of his difficult blocking decisions—had an effect on the landscape.

On his turn, Piazza evoked [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) to kill the opposing [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) , then swung his whole team across at Nelson’s single untapped creature, a lonely-looking [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) . But Nelson had [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) to put [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) back onto the battlefield, blocking Piazza’s 2/1 Finks with his fresh ones and putting [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) in front of [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) . Even doubling up on the Finks’ life gain, though, he had fallen to 9 life. Piazza himself was down to 14, largely because of his own [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) , but his [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher) posed by far the most serious threat on the board.

Nelson’s now petite Finks got in for 2 through Piazza’s tapped-out team. Nelson evoked a [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) to kill Piazza’s looming [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher) , then cast another [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) , putting the life totals at 11–12 in Piazza’s favor.

Both players kept their graveyards splayed out for easy [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) reference—both a courtesy and a convenience. Speaking of Mannequin, Piazza had another one to pull his [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher) back from the grave at the end of Nelson’s turn. Between persist, evoke, Mannequin, and a willingness on both sides to clear the board with trades, the game’s cast of characters had more exits and returns than a soap opera’s.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/16/f3/16f3e5d7c4d16d6e4f4c10fb400646c3d9a9910fd7fee7593f6e115fee478a95.jpg)

Piazza’s [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) and [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher) attacked. Nelson’s Leech traded with Piazza’s, and his [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) stepped in front of [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher) . A [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) from Piazza killed a [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) , but on his turn, Nelson once again had a solution for the 7/7 in [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) , plus his third [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) of the match. He was on 11 (back on it, not still on it), and Piazza was down to 6 off of his own [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher) and [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) and a few hits from Nelson’s Finks.

“11-6,” Nelson dutifully reported when Piazza leaned over to check something.

“I know,” said Piazza. “I was just looking at your graveyard.”

Nelson laughed. “Oh, I wouldn’t do that.”

“I think I know what’s cooo-miiing,” said Piazza in a singsong voice, sending his [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) forward anyway.

But there was no Mannequin, and Piazza’s [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) just traded for Nelson’s 2/1 Finks before he added [Caldera Hellion](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Caldera+Hellion) to the board. Nelson had an end-step [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher) , however, and Piazza, with no answer to the 7/7 and a precarious life total, was dead on the board.

Brad Nelson 1, Brett Piazza 0

“So did you break the mirror?” asked Nelson.

“I think so,” said Piazza.

“Really?” said Nelson. “That would be sick!” After a moment, he added, with audible irony: “I think [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) ’s not that good, personally.”

Piazza took advantage of the new rules allowing outside notes between games, pulling out a folded-up piece of notebook paper covered in reminders on sideboard strategy.

“This is where it gets fun,” said Brad, sideboarding. “This is where the fun starts.”

“I haven’t played this game yet,” said Piazza.

From the sidelines, Conley Woods—having served as consultant for both players—started expounding about the contingent nature of sideboard strategies in the mirror.

“Don’t you think you’re being a little loud there?” said Nelson. “This is a Top 8 match, and you’re right there ....”

Conley held up his hands and subsided at this point, but he was nice enough to share some of his sideboarding insight with me privately after the match.

“There’s no definitive way to do it,” he said of sideboarding for the mirror, and every card in main deck and sideboard has a potential role to play. Basically, he said, there are three approaches, although [Deathmark](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathmark) figures in all of them: There’s the aggro plan (with [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) , [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) , and [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) , but taking out [Volcanic Fallout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Fallout) ), the burn plan (leaving Fallout in and leaning heavily on [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) to win the race), and the control plan (with [Thought Hemorrhage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thought+Hemorrhage) , optional [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) s, and [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) , hoping to kill opposing creatures and win on the back of [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) card advantage). But there are endless nuances and variations depending on the opponent’s plan, and [Thought Hemorrhage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thought+Hemorrhage) gets better as the match progresses.

### Game 2

Piazza, on the play, mulliganed to five, played a single [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) , and then sat on it for turns on end. Nelson summoned [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) —in from the sideboard—and then [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) , with a long cascade revealing more information than he wanted, eventually hitting a useless [Deathmark](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathmark) . Still, he had no room to complain.

A second [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) hit the table, and while Piazza had a Vivid land to let him [Deathmark](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathmark) the [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) , he quickly scooped up both of his lands.

Brad Nelson 2, Brett Piazza 0

Nelson was up another game, but the players joked that Piazza now had an advantage: he knew some of Nelson’s sideboard plan, while his deck had given up almost no information. Neither of them made any changes.

Nelson still seemed concerned at his opponent’s serious demeanor. “Take a breather,” he said. “Pretend you’re at FNM.”

“You just play the game, right?” Piazza replied. “FNM, Top 8 of Nationals ... You just play the game.”

### Game 3

![](https://i.ormos.uk/87/42/8742f3ec328b3682ace49176543809aed37d5428c722615dff954cfb75c4cea5.jpg)*Brad Nelson*”Gimme a sec,” said Nelson, surveying his hand, then: “Nah, I’m good.” Piazza was not so good, sending his hand back once more to stand on six.

As in the first game, the board filled up with a salad of nonbasic lands, and as in the second game, Nelson had a turn-three [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) . This time, though, Piazza had the turn-four [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) .

“No whammies ....” said Nelson. On seeing [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) hit the battlefield, he and Woods, on the sidelines, both cried, “Whammy!”

Nelson had no play on his turn, and Piazza had another [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) . This time he hit his own useless [Deathmark](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathmark) , but Nelson noted with interest that [Caldera Hellion](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Caldera+Hellion) was still in Piazza’s deck, and he’d brought [Thought Hemorrhage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thought+Hemorrhage) in from the board.

Still, Piazza now had three 3/2s on the table. He attacked with all of them, but a timely [Volcanic Fallout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Fallout) from Nelson left him with just a 2/1 [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) . Nelson cast [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) the next turn, and happily blocked the Finks. Piazza had another Finks, to go to 21 vs. Nelson’s 15, but Nelson had [Deathmark](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathmark) to shrink that one as well.

Nelson’s own [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) put him to 19 when it got double- [Deathmark](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathmark) ed, the second one courtesy of Piazza’s third [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) . Nelson fell to 14 on the attack, but his [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) took Piazza to 17, and a Finks of his own left him at 16, the life totals surging up and down like a jumpy stock market.

Piazza’s attack drew a [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) on [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) , putting Nelson up two cards and letting him block and kill Piazza’s team. Piazza came back with a [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) for Nelson’s Finks, and the board was once again empty, like a sensible-sounding metaphor.

Finally it was Nelson’s turn for [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) , hitting an [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) to put the life totals at 18-13 in his favor, then attacked, trading the Elf for [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) and putting Piazza to 10 with the inexorable, untouchable [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) .

Piazza had an Anathemaner of his own, the hard way, to drop Nelson to 13. Nelson swung with both the Stag and his own [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) , but with him at seven mana and Piazza at just five, Piazza’s decision to trade [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) s seemed to inure to Nelson’s advantage. Nelson’s [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) stayed in the graveyard for now in favor of a [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) from Nelson’s hand.

Piazza had another [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) to drop Nelson to 8, but that was all he could do. On his turn, Nelson started doing the math, but Piazza did it faster—Nelson had the 9 damage. “That’s game,” said Piazza, extending the hand.

Brad Nelson 3, Brett Piazza 0

The two discussed their sideboarding plans with Conley as they packed up. Conley told Piazza that he shouldn’t have cast [Deathmark](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathmark) in Game 2; he couldn’t win the game, and it helped Nelson win the sideboarding.

“Sure,” said Piazza. “Both games we played came down to [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) s, though. You had ‘em, I didn’t.

“Yeah,” said Nelson. “The Fallout was huge too.”

“Definitely,” said Piazza.

Nelson now advances to the semifinals to face Adam Yurchick’s Five-Color Control, and apparently he doesn’t like his chances.

Woods disagrees, citing the trio’s near-flawless Standard record and maintaining that the deck “literally beats everything.” The Five-Color Control match-up, he says, is easy if you know how to navigate it. And Nelson has clearly done his homework.

## Semifinals: Charles Gindy (Cruel Control) VS Todd Anderson (Cruel Control)

*by Bill Stark*

“Good luck Todd!” Charles Gindy offered to his opponent in the Semifinals of the United States National Champion.

“Thanks. You too!” Todd Anderson offered back. The two players were battling for a guaranteed slot on the National Team, with the loser off to try one more chance in the 3rd/4th battle. Charles Gindy, a long time pro out of Florida, returned from semi-retirement to take down the top slot at Pro Tour-Hollywood. His opponent, Todd Anderson, hailed from just up the road of Florida in Alabama. He had earned a reputation online as a columnist for Starcitygames.com, and was clearly excited to be playing in the Top 4 of the event. As the players were given the go ahead, however, he turned all serious.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/cc/c3/ccc339c21a36f8447ad76ccd7c433dee15b19f343d55b7377518800fdce7d149.jpg)*Todd Anderson chugs right along through the Top 8 at Nationals.*Things started well for Charlie Gindy, who resolved an early [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) to draw two cards, but things were not as fortunate on the other side of the battlefield. Todd Anderson got to three lands, and then missed back-to-back land drops, forcing him to discard a [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) . When he missed yet again the following turn, he was down yet another card while his opponent had built up a manabase that outnumbered his more than two-to-one. Gindy tried for a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) , but it was countered by [Essence Scatter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Essence+Scatter) . When Todd finally drew a fourth land and attempted [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) , Gindy was ready with [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) .

A second [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) from Charles resolved, but soon died to [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) from Todd. That opened up an opportunity for Gindy, well out-manaing his opponent, to resolve [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) , and a turn later he stuck [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) . The two players fought a mini counter battle over the 4/4, with Charlie coming out on top. Anderson had just five lands but was getting one tapped down each turn via Ajani. He finally managed to pick up a few extra cards with an [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) , but that left him tapped too low and Charlie untapped to resolve [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) . Anderson considered his options facing overwhelming odds, but conceded to get to the second game, rather than limp on through Ultimatum, two Dragons, and an active [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) .

Charles Gindy 1, Todd Anderson 0

“Good luck!” Todd offered his opponent, despite having lost the first game.

After being manascrewed in the first game, Todd Anderson got a slight reprieve in the second as his opponent started on a mulligan. The control mirror meant the games were likely to go long, so being down one card could potentially be a minimal hindrance, but when Charlie sent his second hand of six back for five he had to face the reality he would be overwhelmed by superior advantage from Anderson. As if on cue, Todd piped up with “Looks like this game might go as quickly as the last game.” Gindy offered up a sly smile, clearly not afraid to be on five cards in the Top 8.

Anderson opened on double [Reflecting Pool](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reflecting+Pool) and [Vivid Creek](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vivid+Creek) while Gindy missed his second land drop for a turn. He found one on his next draw step, but was stuck on two while Todd worked his way to five. It was going to be a long game for Charlie Gindy. His odds started looking even longer as Todd resolved an [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) to draw two cards, then cast [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) to net himself both a creature and two more cards. He passed the turn to a Charles Gindy board that featured three lands.
