Charles missed yet another land drop, but was finally able to get onto the board with [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) . Anderson was ready for the 3/3, however, casting [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) and quickly moved the planeswalker to one loyalty counter to kill it. Charles just re-anted with a second copy of the rare, but had to watch disdainfully as his opponent drew yet another two cards with [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) . Looking to go on the beatdown, Todd used [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to tap his opponent’s 3/3 Stag on Charlie’s upkeep, drawing yet another card for doing so, and soon was locking the Stag down each turn with [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) . A second [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) put Todd up two *more* cards, and it seemed all but a lock for the Alabaman.

Gindy tried to soldier on, considering all of his options on his own turn. “I’m not sure how I can win this game...” He muttered, before casting an [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) of his own.

“I have [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) ,” Todd Anderson said, showing the instant from his hand. “I also have [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) ...”

That was all Charlie Gindy needed to see.

Charles Gindy 1, Todd Anderson 1

“I’m going to draw first,” Charlie Gindy said, the decision drawing a small amount of surprise from his opponent. Todd was quick to ship his hand back taking a mulligan. “This is just like my match in Hollywood.” The Pro Tour champ opined. “I thought I was a goner, but then my opponent mulliganed to oblivion.” Gindy finished his thought by playing a land, quickly scooping it back up when he remembered he had put his opponent on the play.

Charlie’s ploy paid off, as the extra card made sure he hit his first four land drops. Todd Anderson, meanwhile, was stuck at three. Gindy closed in, using [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to bounce Todd’s [Vivid Marsh](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vivid+Marsh) , but Anderson had a [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) to counter. Was the play by Charlie just a ploy to resolve a better spell on his turn? It was! He untapped and cast [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) , using the planeswalker to tie up his opponent’s [Vivid Marsh](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vivid+Marsh) , tapped from countering with [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) .

Anderson found a fourth land in the form of [Reflecting Pool](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reflecting+Pool) , but could only say “Yep...” frustratedly as his opponent cast [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) . Anderson attemped [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) to kill the 3/3, but Charlie was ready with a counterspell. “Ugh. Whatever, I can’t win this game,” Todd replied, scooping up his cards with a sense of frustration he took control to keep in check for fear of offending his opponent and the crowd.

Charlie Gindy 2, Todd Anderson 1

“Three games, and six damage dealt!” Charlie Gindy pointed out as they were shuffling for the fourth game of their match. It had not been the drawn out, haymaker filled control mirror many spectators had expected. On the contrary, the match was almost boring with the victor of each game being determined by light mana draws. Almost as soon as the though had permeated the air, Todd Anderson was back to the land of mulligans, shipping his hand for six yet again in the match.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/21/04/2104e4917be3d04487d5d9237c1307836d7fe9674d5e281a8c34c1962e3f351f.jpg)*Charlie Gindy considers his options before making the play.*Happy with that total, Anderson kept rolling the game onwards with three lands in a row and an evoked [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) . He apparently disagreed with his opponent’s play to start on the draw, but which was the right decision would have to be determined by the pace of the game. Gaining access to four mana, Charlie Gindy cast [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) . He cursed under his breath as his opponent cast [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) to kill the creature, but untapped and cast a second copy of the 3/3.

[Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) was Todd Anderson’s solution to that threat, and Gindy evoked a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) to find himself some more gas to keep his game going. “Discard, or land?” Todd Anderson asked his opponent as Gindy shuffled the cards in his hand around nervously. When Gindy failed to play a land, it looked like yet another game determined by a light mana draw from one of the opponents. Todd cast [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) to get his beat on, then countered a second evoked [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) from Charlie Gindy.

Charlie missed yet another land drop, moving to his discard step. Todd responded with [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) to draw two cards, and won the ensuing counter battle. When he untapped and cast [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) , Charlie Gindy had seen enough. Two 4/4s, an [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) on five loyalty counters, and an opposing [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) were all he needed to read the writing on the wall.

Charlie Gindy 2, Todd Anderson 2

“Play or draw?” Todd Anderson asked his opponent as they prepared for the final game. Gindy didn’t answer at first, mulling over his options carefully.

“Ummmmm...I’ll play.” Gindy replied, changing his decision from the last time he’d had the choice. Pro Tour champion or no, it looked like Gindy agreed with Todd Anderson’s strategy in the match.

[Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) hit the table for Charles Gindy on the third turn, while Todd Anderson evoked [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) . With more than seven cards in his hand, he opted to discard a [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) of his own. The play left him tapped out and he could only watch as Gindy cast [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) , drew himself a card, then attacked for 3. It was a big turn for Charlie, but he did miss his fourth land drop again.

Todd considered his plan on his own turn, ultimately opting to use [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to bounce his opponent’s Jace, drawing himself a card in the process. Like a pro, Gindy ripped a [Vivid Creek](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vivid+Creek) from the top of his library, re-cast the Jace, and drew an extra card. His opponent was having none of it, untapping and casting a planeswalker of his own in the form of [Liliana Vess](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Liliana+Vess) . She quickly undid her blue brethren’s efforts, forcing Gindy to discard a [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) . If he couldn’t get rid of the Vess, Charlie’s Jace would actually draw his opponent functionally more cards than its controller if he intended to keep it around.

Gindy found a fifth land waiting for him on his turn, quickly hustling it onto the battlefield and then taking a moment to consider his options. A second [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) was the play, and he attacked with his first copy of the card to knock Liliana down to three loyalty counters. Anderson untapped and played the game’s third planeswalker, [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) . That merited a [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) from Charlie for one, even though Todd could pay the slight tax. Pay he did, using the resolved Ajani to blow up one of the Sable Stags.

His opponent essentially tapped out, Charlie Gindy untapped for a potentially big turn. He activated his Jace to make both players draw a card. He ripped a land with a victorious “Got there!” then flopped [Identity Crisis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Identity+Crisis) onto the table. Todd didn’t look pleased, using his lone mana to [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) for zero. He wanted to stack the top of his library with the clash, but had only a land waiting for him and sent it to the bottom. His draw step yielded [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) to blow up his opponent’s Sable Stag, with Liliana forcing Charlie to discard a third copy from his hand.

The game had swung decidedly in Charlie Gindy’s favor, with an active [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) staring down Todd Anderson’s [Liliana Vess](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Liliana+Vess) . Gindy untapped, found a seventh land, and cast [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) . “That’s a good one...” His opponent replied, marking the totals on his life pad. Thanks to not having any creatures nor cards in hand, the effect of the powerful *Shards of Alara* rare was mitigated slightly; still, a 10-point swing in life totals combined with an Ancestral for Charlie was not a good position for Todd Anderson to be in.

[Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) was waiting for Todd on the top of his library, but in his excitement to draw two cards with it, he neglected to tutor with his [Liliana Vess](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Liliana+Vess) first. He sheepishly admitted the error after making it, passing the turn. Gindy used Jace to make both players draw a card, then undid the effect with an [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) on Todd’s draw step to force his opponent to discard two. Todd tried a counter, but Gindy was ready with a [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) .

He was far ahead on the battlefield, but Charles Gindy needed a threat. [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) was more than willing to step up to the task, entering the battlefield the following turn. [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) from Todd Anderson bounced the Dragon token and drew the player a card, but when he attempted [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) to stop the Broodmate Gindy had [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) .

“Show me a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) and you’ve got it.” Todd Anderson said, resignedly. Gindy flipped over two copies of the card, locking up the match. “Congratulations.” Anderson said, extending his hand with a smile.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/87/75/8775e1348a81e463101bcbb6c9b922dd1e6c50fef7fbc3e8ff75e9d9c1f54c35.jpg)*The opponents amicably end their match with mutual respect.*

Charles Gindy 3, Todd Anderson 2

## Semifinals: Brad Nelson vs. Adam Yurchick

*by Steve Sadin*

Walking around US Nationals you could hear people talking about three decks. Mark Hendrickson’s R/W Control deck that he used to grind into Nationals, and then rip through the swiss rounds before losing to Gindy in the quarterfinals. Shuuhei style 5 color control decks that made up nearly a third of the field, and took up three of the top 4 slots.

And, the real story of the tournament, Conley Woods’ Jund Mannequin deck.

The deck was played by Conley Woods, Brad Nelson and Brett Piazza. This contingent of Magic Online superstars have begun to make their names known on the Pro Tour. Conley posted a top 8 at Pro Tour: Hawaii, Nelson came in 9th at that same Pro Tour (his first Pro Tour to boot) and Brett Piazza has multiple Grand Prix top 8s to his name.

Brett and Brad both top 8ed with the deck and Conley had to settle with a top 16 finish after some bad luck in his second draft pod led to an 0-3 and elimination from top 8 contention.

Yurchick, much like Sam Black at Nationals last year, is a player who has been noticed by the Pro Tour community for quite some time, but has up until this point mostly escaped the public eye. Yurchick can often be found floating near the of the standings at American GPs and has a finals appearance at Grand Prix Philadelphia, a top 4 at Grand Prix Minneapolis and a 9th place on tiebreakers at Pro Tour: Hollywood to his name.

This weekend could very well change that.

Conley’s deck performed incredibly well against 5 color control during the swiss, and Yurchick even lost his first 5 games when he was playtesting the matchup the night before. But, after playing the matchup more and learning the ins and outs of his opponent’s deck, Yurchick felt that the matchup was firmly in his favor.

Nelson agreed that the matchup was in Yurchick’s favor, but he felt that he still had a very strong chance, especially if he drew an early [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) .

### Game 1

Yurchick won the roll, but Nelson made the first play of the match with a turn 3 [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) . Yurchick passed his fourth turn with only a land and Nelson attacked with his Finks knocking Yurchick to 17 before he cast a second Finks. Yurchick laid his fifth land and cast a Hollowed Burial.

Nelson played a [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) for the full 5 mana, but when he went to attack with it the next turn Yurchick had the [Volcanic Fallout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Fallout) . Nelson didn’t have a follow up play and Yurchick used his remaining mana to draw two cards with an [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) . Yurchick then untapped and laid a Jace.

Nelson attempted to [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) the Jace, but Yurchick was ready with a [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) . This left Yurchick without enough mana to offer up an answer to Nelson’s follow up play of [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) .

Yurchick had both players draw an extra card with Jace and then passed the turn with 6 mana up.

Nelson attacked Jace with [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) which Yurchick attempted to block with a flashed in [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) , Nelson had a [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) to get back his [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) to destroy the would be blocking wall, but Yurchick was ready with a second [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) .

Yurchick untapped and cast the gamebreaker of gamebreakers, [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) knocking out Nelson’s hand and leaving himself in a commanding position.

On Yurchick’s next turn he resolved a [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) and then after Nelson’s draw step Yurchick knocked out his cards with an [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) .

![](https://i.ormos.uk/89/67/8967a87a5bcb467c7f137296902e0909a810310954132d06094a4772b92dd0fa.jpg)*Things went from bad to worse for Nelson when Yurchick cast a Broodmate Dragon*

Yurchick untapped, flopped down a second [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) and Nelson immediately conceded.

**Yurchick 1 – Nelson 0**

In between games Yurchick sideboarded out:  
 -3 [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) , -2 [Volcanic Fallout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Fallout) , -2 [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) , -1 Doomblade, -1 [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum)

And sideboarded in:  
 +2 [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) , +2 [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) , +2 [Deathmark](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathmark) , +1 Identinty Crisis, +1 [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) ,+1 [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout)

Nelson sideboarded out:  
 -4 [Volcanic Fallout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Fallout) , -3 [Caldera Hellion](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Caldera+Hellion) , -2 [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) , -3 [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks)

And sideboarded in:  
 +4 [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) , +3 [Thought Hemorrhage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thought+Hemorrhage) , +1 [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher) , +4 [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) e,

### Game 2:

Nelson again made the first play of the game with a turn three [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) . When Nelson attacked Yurchick was ready with a [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) . The [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) successfully blocked, but Nelson had a [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) to give his now 2/1 [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) a free path to attack on the next turn.

Nelson cast a [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag+) on his next turn and Yurchick, unable to counter the popular M10 three drop, could only draw two cards with an [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) .

Yurchick untapped, cast an [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) and chose to add a counter to keep the [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) tapped down instead of destroying the [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) and gaining 3 life.

Nelson immediately recognized that Yurchick was representing [Hallowed Burial](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hallowed+Burial) as he was attempting to keep his [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) alive through the turn.

Nelson, who was holding a [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) and an [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) , both of which were capable of destroying the [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) pondered his options before he decided to attack Yurchick with his [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag+) and use his [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) to destroy the [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) .

Yurchick had the Hollowed Burial to deal with Nelson’s intimidating board, but Nelson was able to rebuild with a [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) revealing [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) .

Things seemed to be looking ok for Nelson until Yurchick untapped and cast a [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) . Nelson sacrificed his [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) and discarded two [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) s.

Yurchick had the [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) , which he had brought back with [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) , for Nelson’s [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) and a [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) for Nelson’s follow up [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) .

An [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) , a [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) and then a [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) allowed Yurchick to take out Nelson before he had a chance to rebuild his position.

**Yurchick 2 – Nelson 0**

Nelson “You do know that you’re probably the sexiest man I’ve ever seen, right?”  
 Yurchick “I get that a lot, it’s probably my diet.”

Tom Lapille, who was watching the match chimed in “I like that Yurchick is actually carrying around a fresh apple.”

Yurchick “I do love my fresh fruit.”

Lapille “It’s true, you haven’t seen Yurchick until you’ve seen him eat an entire grapefruit in under three minutes in-between rounds at a PTQ”

![](https://i.ormos.uk/47/f4/47f44b479f4238263dc1bb28bda93dbe9a7cbbb930ba5861c8021e8db76acee7.jpg)*Remember kids, eat your fruits and vegetables and you too could be on the US National team when you grow up.*

### Game 3

Nelson had a turn 2 [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) , which Yurchick answered with a [Deathmark](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathmark) .

Nelson had a [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) to follow up with and when Yurchick played a third Vivid land tapped, Nelson hit him for an extra three damage with an Anethamancer knocking him down to 14.

Yurchick had a [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) to protect him from [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) s and a [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) to protect his [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) from a [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) .

On Yurchick’s next turn he made Nelson discard his only two cards, a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) and a land with an [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) .

Neslon drew a Makeshit Mannequin to get back his [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) , but Yurchick was more than ready with a [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) .

When Yurchick played a [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) on his next turn Nelson drew, revealed the two lands that he had drawn since the [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) and congratulated Yurchick on making it to the finals.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/02/98/0298f8c29a2082bfff13c6a7178ac6cd4924c35eda93c3f734b08f72d6c43d47.jpg)*Nelson is as gracious in defeat as he is in victory.*

“I hope you take it down man.”

“Thanks. Win your next match, I’d love to have you on the team with me.”

**Yurchick 3 Nelson 0**

## Sunday, July 26, 1:52 p.m. – The Final Fold

*by Kelly Digges*

![](https://i.ormos.uk/47/c1/47c1cbe952d4f5dda4f6b73de642269b6585655275ad0f1c23c8a14d95befd30.jpg)There’s a big crowd gathered to see the final match of 2009 U.S. Nationals. Meanwhile, though, I sought the conclusion to another, quieter drama: Would Legion Events’ Matt Danner finish his level 2 Menger sponge before the end of the event?

The answer, as it turned out, was a resounding yes. Matt proudly showed off the culmination of his solitary effort at the public events registration table. Passersby stopped to marvel at it, and he even gave a few demonstrations on how the cards are folded to curious onlookers.

Now that Matt has spent the whole weekend transforming 2,400 unloved cards into a fractal art project, I had one question. Matt was elsewhere, so I asked tournament organizer Steve Port: What are they going to do with it now?

“We don’t know yet,” said Steve. Several people had expressed interest, he said, but getting it home was the problem. Last year’s sponge hadn’t even made it out of the hall before disintegrating. They’ve got a few potential plans, but if it can’t be saved, Steve’s not going to let its destruction go to waste.

“If nothing else,” he said, “we’ll auction off the right to smash it or something.”

## 3/4 Playoffs: Todd Anderson vs Brad Nelson

*by Tom LaPille*

Brad Nelson is FFfreaK on Magic Online. Not Ffreak, or FFreak, or even FFFreak, but FFfreaK. Nelson started using that handle online when Starcraft released, and for a few years that name has stricken fear into the hearts of men and women on Magic Online. His first Pro Tour was in Honolulu this year, where he placed ninth. That was the first time Nelson’s real name went up in lights, and Todd Anderson was having his own breakout tournament this weekend. Hailing from Alabama, he is a columnist for StarCityGames.com who has played in various Pro Tours but lacks a notable finish.

Nelson was playing a Jund-based [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) deck designed by Pro Tour Honolulu top eight competitor Conley Woods. He lost the semifinals to Adam Yurchick’s five color control deck. Anderson is playing a similar five color control deck that is set up to dominate the late game with powerful spells like [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) and [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) . Nelson will need to use his [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) es, [Boggart Ram-Gang](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boggart+Ram-Gang) s, and Bloodbraid Elves to win games quickly or be buried under six and seven mana spells.

I eavesdropped on Nelson and deck designer Conley Woods while they chatted before the match began, and they believed that the details of Yurchick’s deck were much worse for Nelson than the details of Anderson’s deck. Would Brad Nelson solidify his reputation by taking the third slot on the 2009 United States National Team, or would Todd Anderson walk away from the match with an even brighter first feather in his cap?

### Game 1

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ca/70/ca700a2055dc607beb36e7781cb71d544609becab9fe58dca503a4d4cdb81181.jpg)The first action of the game was a [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) from Nelson that met a [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) . The [Caldera Hellion](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Caldera+Hellion+) that Nelson revealed ended up on the bottom, while the [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) that Anderson kept on top stopped an evoked [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) from Nelson. Anderson dominated the second clash with a [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) that he kept on top for later. Nelson’s second evoked [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) met a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) . Nelson was stuck on three lands, which did not bode well for his chances.

The [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) that Anderson cast with a full five lands was unopposed, then followed up by a vivid land. Would Anderson finish with a [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) next turn? Brad played a tapped land and passed with no action again. Anderson’s seventh land was another vivid land. Nelson finally cast a spell with [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) into [Volcanic Fallout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Fallout) , but that was stymied by a [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) before the [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) that had been waiting in Anderson’s hand since turn four finally hit the table. Nelson’s chances went to near zero.

After the [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) resolved, Anderson forgot to play a land and discarded an [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) , but it didn’t matter. His [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) , [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) , and [Agony Warp](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Agony+Warp) kept Nelson’s [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) from digging him out of the Ultimatum-created hole he was in, and a [Volcanic Fallout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Fallout) for two damage finally put Nelson away.

Anderson 1, Nelson 0

![](https://i.ormos.uk/c4/a8/c4a8f6025167801308b2b6b08b9663e920e2c9e1423251e06aacb894d0f67d4a.jpg)While shuffling for Game 2, Anderson and Nelson betrayedtheir recent arrival to high level competition by exchanging stories from years of grinding at Pro Tour Qualifiers. Despite years of Magic Online play, Nelson had not actually won a qualifier top eight until this year. Anderson had similar tales from this past extended season.

After a little of this, Nelson changed the subject. “My advice for this game is don’t \[forget to play a land and\] discard a land on turn one.”

Anderson frowned. “Thanks.”

### Game 2

Nelson once again had the first action of the game with [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) . Unfortunately, he was short on lands again and an evoked [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) that might have solved his problem was stopped by [Essence Scatter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Essence+Scatter) . His deck produced a fourth land a turn later, which allowed him to [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) the [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) in response to an end step [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) from Anderson. However, Anderson’s [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) emptied Nelson’s board of creatures. Nelson replaced it with a [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) . Both players were still stuck on four lands.

The first player to find a fifth land was Anderson, who cast a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) . While he was tapped out, Nelson hit him with a [Thought Hemorrhage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thought+Hemorrhage) naming [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) . Anderson revealed that he had two in his hand, then thought better of it and conceded rather than allow Nelson to see how he had sideboarded.

Nelson glowed. “Was that as good for you as it was for me?”

Anderson 1, Nelson 1

![](https://i.ormos.uk/3a/93/3a935859755584d381b58a9ee89ab0c426b46457823468256bd7b67d2eb60035.jpg)Nelson sensed weakness in Anderson and needled him while they shuffled. “We’re in the top four, you’re already going to Rome. How can you tilt?” Anderson was quiet. Perhaps Nelson’s years spent terrorizing the Magic Online world as FFfreaK were enough to make him a hardened veteran even after only one Pro Tour.

### Game 3

Nelson started off with a second turn [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) that was stopped by [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) . Anderson was once again mana shy this game as he failed to play a third land, although he had another [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) to stop a [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) and put another unhelpful spell on the bottom. He was rewarded for this with a land, then played [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) naming [Thought Hemorrhage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thought+Hemorrhage) .

A [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) from Nelson hit a [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) . “Aww, that’s no fun.” The Elf got in for three. Anderson drew a [Sunken Ruins](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sunken+Ruins) that let him cast [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) and Lightning Helix the [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) . Nelson’s [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) convinced Ajani to go away next turn. An [Agony Warp](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Agony+Warp) from Anderson killed the [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) , but a second [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) was waiting in the wings to replace it. Anderson paused to make sure the appropriate charge counters were placed on Nelson’s [Vivid Grove](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vivid+Grove) , then cast a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) that he traded for Nelson’s [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) .

One might have guessed from Anderson’s body language that he was far behind, but the only damage he had taken thus far was two attacks from a [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) , and his [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) had gained him three life. At seventeen life with six lands in play, Anderson was in great shape.

Nelson destroyed the [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) with a [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) , then cast a [Thought Hemorrhage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thought+Hemorrhage) .

Nelson: “This is tough.”

Anderson: “Don’t worry, I sided them all out.”

Nelson: “Yeah, sure.”

Anderson: “ [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) .”

It was a miss. Anderson’s hand was [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) , [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) , [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) , and [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) .

Anderson had six lands in play, and a seventh would enable a [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) . “Looking like a land on top to me, boys.” He paused. “Probably a Vivid Land though.” It was neither of those things, but the [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) found a land. Nelson’s Stag knocked Anderson down to 14, then Nelson passed with no action on 7 untapped lands and 3 cards.

Anderson picked up Nelson’s graveyard. “Let’s see what the worst thing you can do to me is.” Anderson was no doubt worried about a [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) in response to his [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) , but he decided to go for the Ultimatum anyway. That’s exactly what Nelson had, and it returned a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) before the Ultimatum promptly killed it again and took half of Nelson’s hand away.

After the Ultimatum, he stated “I’m going to play a land this turn.” It was a [Vivid Creek](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vivid+Creek) . All Nelson could do after the Ultimatum was attack with [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) . At this point, Anderson realized he had eight cards in his hand. A warning was issued and a card was discarded. Then Nelson cast another [Thought Hemorrhage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thought+Hemorrhage) , this time on [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) .

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ce/16/ce16e9b9ec54d742ec0cac50e469dea3781cde6109f6760f2ff9ecb7866425d5.jpg)Nelson was entirely out of gas, although Anderson was going to have to win with [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) s and [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) s rather than the [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) s he had hoped to use. A [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) naming [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) would protect Anderson from any surprises, then a second [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) and a [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) gave Anderson enough of a squad to win with. Nelson had only a [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) . A second Stag from Anderson with a [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) and [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) up put the game away.

Anderson 2, Nelson 1

There was a little more small talk before the fourth game.

Anderson: “I don’t think I’ve ever played against you online.”

Nelson: “I’ve never heard of you.”

Anderson: “I’m ‘Strong Sad’.”

Nelson “Oh.”

### Game 4

Nelson kept quickly, then Anderson spent a moment studying his hand.

Nelson: “What, did you draw eight?”

Anderson: “No, although I did forget to discard earlier.... Nope, not getting there with this one.”

Anderson’s hand of six was good enough to keep. Nelson started with a [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) , then a [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) . Anderson’s [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) killed the Stag and forced Anderson to pump the Leech. A [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) found Nelson more lands, then a [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) found him a second [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) . A second [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) found a [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) , but the only legal targets were his own [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) es. The Pulse disappeared to the bottom of his deck.

Anderson cast a [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) , once again protecting him from Thought Hemmorhage, but he was short on other action. An [Agony Warp](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Agony+Warp) took out one of the Bloodbraid Elves, then the rest of Nelson’s creatures won the game with little fuss.

Anderson 2, Nelson 2

While they shuffled for the final game. Nelson proposed that they play for double or nothing on the semifinalist trophies. Anderson declined, but made a counter-offer.

Anderson: “If I beat you, can I have the lunchbox?”

Nelson: “There is no number of trophies that this lunch box is worth.”

### Game 5

![](https://i.ormos.uk/aa/e6/aae67dde36f31a68369cc09e976abe5908ad695c32d5faa6f4d1b05143f9ff3f.jpg)Nelson mulliganed to six. He still started strong with a turn two [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) but was stymied by a [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) . It revealed a [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) that popped into play next turn. Nelson replaced the countered [Putrid Leech](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Putrid+Leech) , but for the umpteenth time in the match was stuck on three lands with no action. A second [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) from Anderson piled on the pressure, and the only defense that Nelson found was an [Anathemancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Anathemancer) that couldn’t even block the Stags. The pair of elk killed Nelson in only three attacks.

Anderson 3, Nelson 2

Congratulations to Todd Anderson for making the 2009 United States national team.

## Finals: Charles Gindy (Cruel Control) vs. Adam Yurchick (Cruel Control)

*by Brian David-Marshall*

![](https://i.ormos.uk/fe/05/fe05ac0a924a2c7228a76a8cfa7156dd3a16d2a8932f310358e1c9f50c941900.jpg)Having locked up National team membership with their semifinal victories the pressure was off for both of these Pro Tour veterans, who were assessing the dreaded 3rd/4th matchup between Todd Anderson and Brad Nelson. Like every other National team they were imagining how they would match up against the Japanese. Someone pointed out that there was talk that the third player on the Japanese team, Yuma Shiota, might not be attending Worlds due to a demanding work schedule.

“Was Kenji fourth?” asked Adam Yurchick, who had not dropped a game yet in the Top 8 and was playing with tremendous confidence, to a chorus of bobbing heads. “That’s gross. At least I never lose against Shuhei.”

Meanwhile Gindy was getting the lowdown on how Adam played the control mirror.

“He always has a turn seven [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) ,” said a friend who had watched the semis. “That’s what you need to know.”

As they sat down to play they high-fived.

“Its what we predicted!” exclaimed Gindy who was looking around for Tim Aten, who had a special card for him. “I want my Broodmate token.”

![](https://i.ormos.uk/2a/e2/2ae2358b1d24aa3df4949d72dd4664bbf2dfe2f356c43523a045ee366d8d1be3.jpg)It was a Tim Aten player card that Tim had customized with a word balloon: “You’ll have 2 dragons out, and you’ll prolly still lose!”

“Tim just wants me to know how bad I suck,” laughed Gindy.

“Remember Hollywood when we were ranked first and second in the World?,” asked Gindy who won that Pro Tour. Adam finsihed ninth in that event. “Where do you think we are now?”

### Game 1

Adam mulliganed but kept his next six. Gindy started on seven. Adam played the first of many Vivid lands and, despite ten D6 on the table, looked for more. “Does anyone have dice?”

He knew they would be settling in for a long game -- especially with so many dead Game 1 cards. Each player had [Volcanic Fallout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Fallout) s, [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) s, [Hallowed Burial](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hallowed+Burial) s, and additional spot removal to bias their decks against the Elves, Kithkin, Faeries, and Jund decks through the Swiss rounds. Both players had four lands when Adam cracked the seal with an [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) to draw two cards. He attempted to stick a Jace but it was [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) d. Gindy was content to play draw-go and just used [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) at the end of Adam’s turn while they both played lands and the occasional [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) .

Deep into the “action” Gindy was ahead 13 to 9 -- on lands that is. The life totals had not been scratched yet. This is the most exciting match,” groaned Gindy. “So exciting.”

Rather than discard, Adam played [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) on his own [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) bringing the land totals to 15 to 12 for Gindy. It was 16 to 13 when Adam decided to go “aggro” and asked Gindy to discard two cards with [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) . Gindy pitched [Essence Scatter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Essence+Scatter) and Fallout. Adam did it again and this time Gindy decided to [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) that one. That drew [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) from Adam and another [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) from Gindy. Adam played [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) for three and Gindy paid and then drew two from the Commands on the stack.

Adam untapped to play the [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) that the previous fight had been the undercard to. There were two cards left in Adam’s hand and Gindy had five mana to attempt the third [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) . Adam played [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) for two and revealed Ajani for Gindy and land for Adam. He got back a previously pitched Dragon. Gindy could only keep one card and pitched [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) , [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) , and [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) . Gindy Untapped to play the revealed Ajani with one mystery card left in hand.

Adam played [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) and the card turned out to be Gindy’s fourth [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) . Adam shrugged, played [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) , and got the Dragon back to try again next turn. Ajani went up to five loyalty keeping a [Sunken Ruins](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sunken+Ruins) submerged for Gindy but the best he could hope for was a fog effect from his Planeswalker in the long run as Adam’s next attempt at Broodmate was succesful.

“I don’t get the Tim token?” asked Adam.

“No, you don’t get the Tim one,” pouted Gindy who ticked Ajani’s loyalty up to six before she fell to the twin strike from the dragons. Gindy tried to climb back into the hand business with [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) into [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) . Adam played [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) on the elemental for 10. Gindy X-spelled back for 6. Adam had [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) .

“Do you have anything left?” asked Gindy who was at 10 before the next dragon hit. “Do you have a Fallout?”

Adam flashed him [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) .

“Thank you.”

“I just drew it.”

Charles Gindy - 0 Adam Yurchick - 1

### Game 2

Gindy’s sideboard plan was as follows:

-2 [Hallowed Burial](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hallowed+Burial)  
 -1 [Essence Scatter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Essence+Scatter)  
 -3 [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil)  
 -4 Fallout  
 -1 Dragon

+4 [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag)  
 +2 [Identity Crisis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Identity+Crisis)  
 +2 [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren)  
 +1 [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate)  
 +2 [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo)

Adam’s was:

-3 [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil)  
 -2 [Volcanic Fallout](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volcanic+Fallout)  
 -1 Doomblade  
 -2 [Hallowed Burial](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hallowed+Burial)  
 -1 [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile)

+2 Ajani Venjeant  
 +2 [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag)  
 +2 [Identity Crisis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Identity+Crisis)  
 +1 [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate)  
 +2 [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo)

![](https://i.ormos.uk/15/9d/159d6b029971640939f7b5f91f150a7b045d24994c2cbb8af1591464dda74d52.jpg)Gindy took a page out of Adam’s book with a discard mode [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) at the end of Adam’s fourth turn. Adam pitched a land and Ajani. Gindy missed his fifth land drop and played a sideboarded [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) . Adam untapped and killed the Stag with Ajani.

“Another one? Really?” said Gindy who knew there were only two copied of Ajani in Adam’s deck. Adam began to play draw-grow with Ajani while Gindy dug for more cards with [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) . Gindy offered up [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) and Adam cast [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to counter it and bounce one of Gindy’s untapped vivid lands. Gindy did not have the [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) and replayed the land tapped. Adam untapped into [Identity Crisis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Identity+Crisis) and grew Ajani. They played draw-grow for a while -- Ajani was two turns away from going ultimate. Adam attempted another [Identity Crisis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Identity+Crisis) but it drew [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) from Gindy. All his lands went away a turn later.

Adam also played Jace. He did not have a way to start doing damage on the board but he was in total command. Gindy played three straight lands but Adam was nine up on him. Gindy evoked [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) . Adam untapped and decided to protect himself from [Identity Crisis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Identity+Crisis) with [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) . Gindy tried to banish Jace with his own copy but Adam had the [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) . Both players summoned [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) .

Adam drew a pair of cards expending his Jace. He made Gindy ditch two of his three cards -- including a blank [Identity Crisis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Identity+Crisis) -- and Adam played another Jace. Gindy was [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) ed in his draw step and Gindy said, “Show me a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) ...”

Adam showed it to him along with [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) and [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) .

“I still had all of theeeeeese....” said Gindy with an eye roll.

  
 Charles Gindy - 0 Adam Yurchick - 2

![](https://i.ormos.uk/f1/33/f1335b143dbac51ec69974fbe6a864b8c503006630c3e0e8bc9a6d00aa64112f.jpg)

### Game 3

Adam swapped out one Burial for another Stag and there were no changes for Gindy

“How’s it going over there,” shouted Brad Nelson who was in a two to one hole in his match with Todd Anderson.

“Yurchick is killing me!” shouted Gindy.

“I just realized I messed up my sideboarding,” clucked Adam, as the two players pitched back their opening hands. “I am so used to these games going back and forth I thought I was on the play.”

Gindy resolved Jace on turn three and Adam played a Stag. Gindy was about to lose Jace to the Stag but set Adam back by [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) ing a [Vivid Meadow](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vivid+Meadow) . Gindy paid full price for [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) .

The two players traded cantrip Boomerangs in a battle over reources but Gindy was ahead in hand and on the board with a pair of [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) -- and [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) on Sable Stag. The two fliers took Adam down to 12. He [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) ed for two cards EOT but passed the turn back to Gindy who did the same. Adam fell to 8 from the fliers. He drew two more cards as Gindy discarded [Broodmate Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broodmate+Dragon) . Jace stuck for Adam. Both players drew. Gindy ignored the Planeswalker and dropped Adam to 4 who was digging frantically with another [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) . Adam attempted an Ajani but it was [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) d. Adam attempted to buy another turn with an upkeep [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) but when Gindy started tapping mana Adam began getting ready for Game 4. At least he was sideboarded correctly for it.

  
 Charles Gindy - 1 Adam Yurchick - 2

### Game 4

Adam mulliganed a two-land hand with a quick peek at the [Identity Crisis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Identity+Crisis) on top of his deck. Gindy kept his seven. Nothing happened until the ceremonial first [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) was thrown out at the end of turn five by Gindy. He untapped and attempted to land an Ajani. He had the [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) for Adam’s [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) and Adam’s [Reflecting Pool](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reflecting+Pool) was locked down. Adam peeled back the top card of his deck -- he needed it to be a land that did not come into play tapped -- and he was rewarded. Gindy staggered under the body blow of [Identity Crisis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Identity+Crisis) .

“That might have been a blow out,” declared a spectator along the rail.

Adam tried [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) but the one card in Gindy’s hand was a timely [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) . Adam played a [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) and protected it from a Cruel fate with [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) . He could not do anything about Ajani zapping it though. Both players were on one card after Adam played [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) on Ajani. They both played Sable Stags but Gindy still had a supply of Lightning Helixes from his Planeswalker and he began the long road back through the red zone. Adam went to 8 from the Stag. He tried [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) but Gindy had [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) -- revealing [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) to Adam’s Broodmate. “I am going to keep.” Ajani ticked up to 7 loyalty and was a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) away from blowing up all of Adam’s land and forcing a game 5.

Adam attempted Broodmate but Gindy had [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) and drew a card instead of bouncing the Ajani -- Adam had enough land to replay it. Adam made Gindy discard two during his draw step but still had no answer to the Stag which dropped him to five. He fell to 2 a turn later. Adam played Cryptic to tap and draw a card during Gindy’s upkeep. Gindy drew a card and beamed as he pointed the freshly drawn [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) at the Runed halo.

“Wooooooow!” exhaled Gindy who had all but resigned himself to losing after the [Identity Crisis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Identity+Crisis) struck. He beamed at Adam, who seemed stunned to have lost. “We have to make it interesting for the crowds”

“I could have done some things differently,” sighed Adam.

Charles Gindy - 2 Adam Yurchick - 2

### Game 5

The two players shook hands and dove into the deciding game. Adam deliberated if he wanted to play for the National Championship with a hand of three lands, Ajani, [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) , and two Cruel. Ultimately he decided that he did. The players traded [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) s to bounce lands in the early turns. Adam landed an [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) and kept Gindy’s [Cascade Bluffs](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cascade+Bluffs) tapped. Adam kept the Bluffs tapped and played a Stag. Gindy bounced the Ajani with [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) and Adam chose not to fight over it with three lands open.

He swung with the Stag for first blood but Gindy was winning the only battle that seemed to matter in this match-up; who could get the most lands in play. He was up 8 to 6 on Adam who used [Esper Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Esper+Charm) for two cards. He played a second Stag. Gindy used a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to bounce a land at end of Adam’s turn. Gindy was setting up to have the mana advantage going into a battle over [Identity Crisis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Identity+Crisis) . He hit land number nine and fired the first salvo. Adam tapped out for [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) and Gindy cast [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) -- revealing [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) -- to force it through.

Adam attacked Gindy down to 8 and played Jace ripped right off the top of his deck, which found him a sixth land -- but Gindy was on cruise control now. He legend ruled the Jace and played [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) .  
 Adam attacked with his remaining Stag to get Gindy to 10. Another [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) and [Great Sable Stag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Sable+Stag) was Gindy’s next turn. A pair of [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) came down a turn later for Gindy naming Cruel and [Identity Crisis](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Identity+Crisis) . Gindy flashed Adam yet another [Cruel Ultimatum](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cruel+Ultimatum) . Adam was unmoved. Gindy showed him [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20210305044841/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) .

That was enough and Adam extended the hand. While the two competitors were in the post game ritual Tim Aten marched up and took back his token.

“Hey! That was mine!” declared Gindy who soon forgot about the token as it began to sink in that he had just won the 2009 U.S. National Championship. He would add that trophy to a mantle that already had awards for winning a Grand Prix and a Pro Tour.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/83/f6/83f6f72d7d73f4012db8389d3be2ed20021e91422aabb6b2f2615941b76bbd71.jpg)

Congratulations to the 2009 U.S. National Champion Charles Gindy and to the rest of the team; Adam Yurchick, Todd Anderson, and alternate Brad Nelson.
